experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/getting-oriented/codebase-map.mdx:332:| <RepoFile path="crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs">crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs</RepoFile> | Bollard-based Docker API client — `DockerApi` trait (container/network/image/exec operations), `BollardDockerClient` (production), `FakeDockerClient` (tests), and `ContainerState` enum (all 9 Docker lifecycle states). `BollardDockerClient::connect()` honors a non-empty `DOCKER_HOST` (matching Docker CLI's empty-as-unset semantics); otherwise it asks `docker context inspect` for the active CLI context endpoint (passing any `DOCKER_CONTEXT` override explicitly) and caches the result in a process-wide `OnceLock` so typed API calls and remaining Docker CLI calls target the same daemon without re-spawning the subprocess on every connect. This path supports local-socket and plain HTTP/TCP contexts; SSH and TLS contexts fail clearly instead of dropping Docker CLI transport/TLS settings that this Bollard build cannot mirror. Replaces Docker CLI string-matching for lifecycle, cleanup, and inspect paths. CLI stays CLI for `docker build` (no parsing risk, streaming context complexity), `docker exec -it` / `docker run -it` (TTY-bound; bollard exposes the raw stream but the Docker CLI manages raw-terminal mode, Ctrl-C signal forwarding, and SIGWINCH for free), and `docker run --rm` one-shot commands (fire-and-forget captured stdout; no parsing risk). |

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/getting-oriented/architecture.mdx:19:jackin❯ deliberately uses your existing Docker engine as the operator control plane. For host-side Docker API calls, jackin❯ honors `DOCKER_HOST` when the operator sets it to a non-empty value (matching Docker CLI's own treatment of an empty `DOCKER_HOST=` as unset), including TLS endpoints — when `DOCKER_HOST` points at a `tcp://` daemon and `DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY` / `DOCKER_CERT_PATH` are set, jackin❯ performs the mutual-TLS handshake with those client certificates, so a remote daemon or a Docker-in-Docker socket on `:2376` works out of the box. Otherwise it inspects the active Docker CLI context — including any per-shell `DOCKER_CONTEXT` override — and connects to that context's Docker host when the context is a local socket or plain HTTP/TCP endpoint, so API calls such as network creation land on the same daemon that later `docker run` commands use. SSH Docker contexts are rejected for now because the Bollard dependency build cannot reproduce the Docker CLI's SSH transport, and TLS *contexts* are rejected because jackin❯ does not yet wire a context's TLS material into the connection (a directly-set `DOCKER_HOST` with explicit `DOCKER_CERT_PATH` is the supported path for TLS daemons).

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/getting-oriented/architecture.mdx:114:`jackin hardline` inspects the target container. experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/roadmap/(isolation-security)/open-review-findings.mdx:22:- **Secret redaction in command failures (real leak, not just a re-check).** `ShellRunner` in <RepoFile path="crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/shell_runner.rs" /> only redacts `-e`/`--env` argv values in the `--debug` log line (`log_command`, `redact_env_args`) and in the `tee_to_build_log` command string. Launch (<RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/launch.rs" />) pushes `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, and other secrets as `-e KEY=value` pairs into that same `args` slice, so a failing `docker run` surfaces the raw token in the CLI/terminal-visible error text. Fix: route every failure-path error string through `redact_env_args` the same way the debug log path already does.

Schema `v1alpha8` carries the `dirty_exit_policy` config (global + per-workspace: `ask` / `keep` / `discard`).

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/capsule/index.mdx:145:The interactive attach path is long-lived. The short-lived `docker exec` client connects, sends `Hello`, then forwards operator terminal input as `Input` frames and terminal resizes as `Resize` frames. The daemon replies with `Welcome`, then streams rendered terminal output as `Output` frames. When the operator detaches, the client sends `Detach` and exits; the daemon keeps every PTY running. When the last live session ends, the daemon sends `Shutdown`, drains the final frame, exits as PID 1, and Docker reports the role container stopped.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/roadmap/(agent-orchestration)/(distributed-work)/jackin-remote.mdx:11:jackin❯ is no longer purely single-backend in the schema: host config has `[runtime].default_backend`, workspace config has `[runtime].backend`, launch resolution accepts `docker` and `apple-container`, and instance manifests now carry optional backend resources for non-Docker launches. The default Docker backend still owns the normal role-container plus private DinD lifecycle, and `apple-container` is a local backend path rather than a remote host story.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/tui/dialogs.mdx:154:**Confirmed quit and `Ctrl+C` both exit immediately in the launch cockpit.** In the launch cockpit, `Ctrl+Q` opens the confirmation, and choosing Yes is a *hard* stop: the terminal is restored and the process exits immediately. It runs **no cleanup** and waits on no in-flight work (a slow `docker build`, a `spawn_blocking` binary download). `Ctrl+C` bypasses the confirmation and takes the same hard-stop path. Any docker resources left behind are reclaimed by the next launch's `gc_orphaned_resources`. This is why these exits cannot hang on a slow stage: they do not wait for anything.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/roadmap/(operator-surface)/clipboard-image-bridge.mdx:15:Operator-facing usage and validation instructions live in [Host Affordances](/guides/host-affordances/), covering `JACKIN_HOST_ATTACH=1`, image paste, explicit image-path staging, GitHub PR/CI opening, visible URL opening, file export, and the live validation checklist. The binary attach protocol lives in `jackin-protocol`, shared by the host runtime and Capsule; `jackin-capsule attach-proxy` relays raw attach bytes for backends (Docker Desktop, OrbStack) where the host cannot open the mounted Unix socket directly, and `jackin-runtime` picks direct socket vs proxy transport automatically. `JACKIN_HOST_ATTACH=1` opts foreground attach paths (`hardline`, reconnect, new shell/agent sessions) into the host-owned attach loop; the default path stays the in-container `docker exec -it` client until live validation proves parity.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/reference/tui/chrome.mdx:269:The in-container capsule client terminal adapter (<RepoFile path="crates/apps/jackin-capsule/src/tui/terminal.rs">crates/apps/jackin-capsule/src/tui/terminal.rs</RepoFile>) would otherwise toggle its **own** alternate screen (`?1049h`/`?1049l`) over the `docker exec` channel, so detaching it would pop the operator back to the cooked terminal even though the host still holds a screen. Standalone capsule invocations (`jackin hardline`) do not set the env, so the client manages its own screen exactly as before.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/commands/prewarm.mdx:12:`jackin prewarm` fills jackin-owned runtime caches before a later launch asks for them. It prefetches agent runtime binaries and the matching `jackin-capsule` binary under `~/.jackin/` cache state. With `--sidecar`, it ensures the `docker:dind` sidecar image is present before a fresh launch needs to start a private Docker daemon; that sidecar-image check runs alongside binary cache warming instead of waiting until every runtime row has printed. With `--sidecar-container`, it starts a disposable `docker:dind` sidecar through the same network, image lookup/pull, TLS certificate, readiness, and cleanup path fresh launches use, waits until it is ready, prints the ready latency in milliseconds, then removes the container, network, and cert volume. Add `--keep-sidecar-container` to leave that explicitly prewarmed jackin-owned sidecar container, network, and cert volume running; a later fresh launch can acquire the prewarm-adoption lock, adopt the ready resources as a one-shot warm sidecar, and record the actual Docker resource names in the instance manifest so normal cleanup, eject, and purge remove them. `--daemon` is the shorthand daemon-oriented surface for that kept-sidecar path: it warms and keeps the private Docker daemon without running the duplicate standalone sidecar-image prewarm first. With `--roles`, it clones or updates configured role repos under `~/.jackin/roles/`; add `--role <SELECTOR>` to target one role repo, `--workspace <NAME>` to target that saved workspace's default role repo, or `--all-workspaces` to target the default role repos used by saved workspaces. With `--image`, it also checks or builds derived Docker image tags for a role by running the same image recipe validity decision used by launch, records the reuse-or-rebuild decision for the current invocation, and warms the `docker:dind` sidecar image needed by a later fresh start. Add `--all-roles` to image-prewarm every configured role source without relying on saved workspace defaults.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/commands/prewarm.mdx:14:It does not mutate workspaces, edit host git config, edit shell config, write `gh` config, or change agent configs outside jackin-owned state. Image prewarm may clone or update jackin-owned cached role repos under `~/.jackin/roles/`, create temporary jackin-owned Docker build contexts, and run Docker builds when image labels are missing or stale. `--sidecar-container` creates only jackin-owned Docker resources and removes them before returning unless `--keep-sidecar-container` is passed explicitly; kept resources stay under jackin-owned Docker labels and are consumed by a later fresh launch rather than silently mutating host repos, git config, shell config, `gh` config, or agent configs.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/commands/prewarm.mdx:28:| `--image` | Also prewarm derived Docker image(s) for a role and the `docker:dind` sidecar image needed by fresh starts. Valid local image labels are reused; missing or invalid recipes are rebuilt, and multiple selected agent image targets run concurrently while output stays sorted. |

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/security-model.mdx:177:Docker-in-Docker (DinD) is a grant, not an always-on part of each role. Standard, Hardened, and Locked default to no DinD; Compat defaults to privileged DinD. A role's effective grant may enable rootless or privileged DinD. When enabled, jackin❯ launches a per-role sidecar, generates TLS certificates, shares the client certs with the role container through a Docker volume, and points its Docker client at the TLS endpoint (`DOCKER_HOST=tcp://...:2376`, `DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY=1`).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/security-model.mdx:183:Docker profiles provide `none`, `allowlist`, and `open` egress modes. Hardened and Locked default to allowlist; Standard and Compat default to open. `none` disables Docker networking only when DinD is off. DinD requires a non-internal per-role network, and the firewall is installed only for `allowlist`, so `none` with DinD does not isolate role egress. Wrapped agent and shell sessions do not receive root or `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, so the `sudo` and `user = "root"` grant metadata does not let them flush the firewall. Inner containers started through any DinD grant bypass the role container's host-iptables rules, including rootless DinD.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/security-model.mdx:224:| Agent reaches host Docker daemon | jackin❯ does not mount the host Docker socket by default; an explicit mount or reachable API endpoint can expose a daemon. With a DinD grant, the role uses a separate per-role endpoint over TLS | Host Docker starts the sidecar; privileged DinD can bypass some role-container capability drops. Rootless DinD skips `--privileged`; any DinD tier lets inner containers bypass host-iptables rules |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/security-model.mdx:226:| Agent interferes with another agent | Different role launches use separate containers; when networking is enabled, their Docker networks are separate. Runtimes or tabs within one launch share those resources | On Docker, role containers share the daemon host's Linux kernel; Apple Container runs workloads inside VMs |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/security-model.mdx:234:Docker containers share the Linux kernel used by their Docker daemon; Docker isolation is not a hypervisor boundary. experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/docker-profiles.mdx:48:- Roles whose images are purpose-built (all tools pre-installed at `docker build` time)
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/guides/docker-profiles.mdx:177:When the resolved `system_writes` grant is `false`, Docker starts the container with `--read-only` and writable in-memory scratch space for startup paths. This is the default for `locked` and `hardened`. `standard` and `compat` resolve to a writable root filesystem, and grants only widen that setting, so `system_writes = false` cannot narrow those profiles. `true` removes the Docker-level read-only mount. Per-session Landlock protections apply outside explicitly admitted cwd and workspace mounts; the runtime grants `FULL` access to those paths and retains DAC override. For example, `workdir = "/usr"` is accepted with the workspace mounted at `/usr/project`; on a writable-root profile, the admitted `/usr` cwd lets the session write under `/usr`. System package installation from agent sessions is unsupported; install system tools in the role image.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/concepts.mdx:66:From an operator's point of view, the Capsule is what makes a running instance feel like a persistent agent workspace instead of a one-shot `docker run`:
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/concepts.mdx:108:The sidecar is launched by the Docker engine, and containers created through it share the Linux kernel used by that daemon. On macOS, Docker Desktop and OrbStack run the daemon inside a Linux VM, so this is the backend VM's kernel rather than macOS's. jackin❯ does not mount the host Docker socket by default, but an explicit workspace mount can expose it. A privileged DinD grant can bypass some role-container capability drops; when allowlist egress is selected, any DinD grant lets nested containers bypass the role's host-iptables rules. DinD provides a separate Docker API endpoint, not a VM boundary.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:33:On the Docker backend, each loaded role runs inside a `Docker` container. Agent runtimes or tabs within one role share that container, its filesystem, process namespace, configured network namespace, and workspace mounts. Per-instance home/auth mount paths and credential bindings remain scoped to each admitted instance. A per-role `Docker-in-Docker` (`DinD`) sidecar is available when its effective grants enable it. Jackin❯ supplies this private Docker daemon only with that grant. It does not add the host Docker socket as a standard mount; configured mounts or reachable Docker APIs may expose another daemon. From *your* perspective as the operator, configured mounts and grants define what the role can access.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:46:   By default, the role container directly sees mounted host paths; a reachable host Docker API or container escape can bypass this boundary. jackin❯ does not add the host Docker socket as a standard mount. An enabled DinD grant points the role at a separate private sidecar daemon; configured mounts or reachable APIs may expose other host services.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:144:- **Isolation depends on the backend** — Docker containers share the Linux kernel used by their Docker daemon and are not a VM boundary. On macOS with Docker Desktop or OrbStack, that daemon runs inside a Linux VM, so containers share its kernel rather than macOS's. Apple Container runs workloads inside VMs. jackin❯ remains unsafe research software, not a validated boundary for hostile workloads. Additional isolation is explored in [isolation research](/research/platform/isolation/) and tracked as unfinished work on the [Roadmap](/roadmap/).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:146:- **Not Kubernetes** — jackin❯ runs on one local machine with Docker or Apple Container. Apple Container requires macOS 26 ARM and the `apple/container` CLI v0.11.0 or later. Kubernetes support is on the roadmap for teams that need multi-node orchestration.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:148:These limits are deliberate. Docker remains the most established backend, and Apple Container provides a VM-backed local option. The project is focused on local development; the same operator and workspace model may grow toward other sandbox backends and Kubernetes-backed debug workflows.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/(public)/getting-started/why.mdx:183:Other isolation options remain a research direction. The Docker backend uses containers; Apple Container runs workloads in VMs. The roadmap tracks a **Docker runtime hardening contract** for profiles, DinD hardening, resource budgets, and launch-contract reporting; an **OrbStack isolated machine backend** for macOS operators who want a stronger local boundary while preserving Docker workflows; **smolvm backend research** for an open Rust/libkrun VM path; and **reproducibility/provenance pinning** so what runs is what was reviewed. The plan is to keep iterating on UX and ergonomics until jackin❯ is no longer in the *experimenting* phase, and then add stronger boundaries as configurable options per workspace — never as a single forced choice.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/product/operator-experience/host-affordances/host-affordance-bridge-design/03-architecture-and-security.mdx:30:- Uses a stdio relay fallback through `docker exec -i` when direct host connection to the Unix socket is impossible, especially Docker Desktop and OrbStack on macOS.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/product/operator-experience/host-affordances/host-affordance-bridge-design/03-architecture-and-security.mdx:41:jackin❯ already bind-mounts a per-instance host directory at `/jackin/run`; the Capsule socket appears at `~/.jackin/sockets/<container>/jackin.sock`. On same-kernel Docker hosts, host code can open that socket directly. On Docker Desktop for macOS, the host can see the inode through the bind mount but cannot connect to the Linux VM's Unix socket reliably. OrbStack is Docker-compatible but has its own macOS/VM filesystem and socket integration behavior, so it had to be tested as a separate local container backend rather than assumed equivalent to Docker Desktop.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/product/operator-experience/host-affordances/host-affordance-bridge-design/03-architecture-and-security.mdx:48:| Docker Desktop macOS | Host process runs `docker exec -i <container> /jackin/runtime/jackin-capsule attach-proxy`; the proxy connects to `/jackin/run/jackin.sock` inside the container and relays framed bytes over stdio. |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/product/operator-experience/host-affordances/host-affordance-bridge-design/03-architecture-and-security.mdx:49:| OrbStack macOS | Probe direct host connection to `~/.jackin/sockets/<container>/jackin.sock`; if connect fails, use the same `docker exec -i ... attach-proxy` relay. The implementation records which path was selected in debug logs. |

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:33:- **A non-expert operator recovers from an infrastructure crash with a couple of `jackin` commands — no Docker hacks.** This is the load-bearing goal everything else serves. When the Docker daemon disappears out from under live sessions — an OrbStack (or Docker Desktop) update swapping its VM, a host power-off, a laptop sleep that stops the engine, a `docker stop` on shutdown — the operator should open `jackin console`, see the interrupted sessions listed as resumable, pick one, and be back where they were. They must never have to run `docker ps -a`, `docker start`, read exit codes, or inspect socket directories to get their work back. Recovery is a jackin❯ flow a beginner can follow, not a Docker rescue operation an engineer performs. Restore brings back as much as the surviving host state allows, and says plainly what (if anything) could not return.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:37:- Resuming rebinds the **same instance** — same `container_base`, same `~/.jackin/data/<container>/` — so home, plugins, auth, conversation, and the isolated worktree/clone return in place. A power-off or `docker rm` that removed the container but left the host data directory is fully recoverable.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:38:- Restore **degrades by tier and reuses before it rebuilds**: reconnect to a still-running container, `docker start` a stopped one, recreate a deleted one from the stored launch recipe reusing the same image, and rebuild the image only when it too is gone — pinned to the launch-time snapshot so the operator returns to the state that was working, never a re-resolved different one.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:46:- **Durable per-instance host state.** `~/.jackin/data/<container>/` survives `docker rm`: `.jackin/instance.json` (the manifest), `.jackin/isolation.json` (mount records), `home/` (every agent runtime's home, including Claude plugins and `.claude.json`), the per-agent auth slots, and the materialised `git/worktree/` and `git/clone/` trees. See [Runtime Instance Model](/reference/runtime/runtime-instance-model/).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:75:| F16 | Medium | **A daemon/VM mass-kill mislabels healthy sessions.** When the Docker daemon dies under N live `active` sessions (OrbStack upgrade, host power-off, `docker stop` on shutdown), the role containers exit `255` and the next inspect stamps each one `failed_setup` — a status reserved for "the setup hook failed", not "the host went away". It is a restore candidate by luck (`is_restore_candidate()` includes `FailedSetup`), but the label is wrong and the index is never reconciled against the true cause. No distinct `InstanceStatus` variant for "killed by host" exists at the evidence cutoff. This is the involuntary sibling of F12 (stale `active` rows) and wants the same launch-time reconciliation, plus an honest terminal status for "killed by host" distinct from "failed setup". |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:104:3. **Resume** rebinds the same instance and walks the **restore ladder** (see *Restore model*): reconnect to a still-running container, `docker start` a stopped one, or recreate a deleted one from the stored launch recipe reusing the same image — rebuilding only when the image is gone. Home, plugins, auth, conversation, and the same worktree/clone return in place; only secret values are re-resolved. Verify-and-acknowledge runs before any worktree/clone is reused.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:187:                Tier 1  Stopped/exists    → docker start + reconnect
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:188:                Tier 2  NotFound, image   → docker run, reuse stored image_tag
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:277:`restore_available` is the durable "container gone, host state survives" state — reached by an explicit `Keep`, and also the state a power-off or external `docker rm` should resolve to once index reconciliation (F12) runs. `resume` rebinds it back to `active` against the same identity.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:289:  ├─ Stopped / exists ─► Tier 1  docker start + reconnect — same container, same writable layer
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:290:  ├─ NotFound, image present ─► Tier 2  docker run reusing the stored image_tag — no build
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:297:| 1 | container **Stopped**, still exists | re-materialize `agent.toml`, start `-dind` (await healthy), then `docker start` + reconnect | the literal container, full writable layer intact |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:298:| 2 | **NotFound**, image present | `docker run` reusing the stored `image_tag` | a functionally equal container; bind-mounted home/conversation intact |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:301:Tier 0 and Tier 1 are the highest fidelity and the common case for the operator's scenario — battery died, machine restarted, `docker stop` on shutdown, OrbStack upgrade — because the container's writable layer is untouched, so everything the agent installed *inside* it (shell history, `oh-my-zsh`, ad-hoc tools, anything not bind-mounted) comes back exactly. Tier 1 today (`start_or_reconnect_capsule_client`) calls `docker start` and reconnects but does **not** call `prepare_socket_dir` first (F15): a bare `docker start` fails if `/jackin/run/agent.toml` — the capsule launch config, bind-mounted from the non-durable `~/.jackin/sockets/<container>/` — is ever missing, and the `-dind` sidecar must be running before the role container boots. Tier 1 must therefore re-materialize `agent.toml` from the stored recipe and bring the sidecar up first, then `docker start`. Tier 2 reuses the persisted `image_tag` as-is. Tier 3 is the only tier that builds, and it must reproduce the **same** image — pinned to the role commit and base image recorded at first launch, not the current `HEAD` (F14).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:313:| docker run flags / network / DinD shape | partially (`DockerResources`) | replay the same shape |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:314:| capsule launch config — `/jackin/run/agent.toml` (workdir, agent key, session env) | written to the socket dir at fresh launch only, **not durable** (teardown empties it, and Tier 1 `docker start` never rewrites it) | regenerate into `~/.jackin/sockets/<container>/` before Tier 1 `docker start` / Tier 2 `docker run`, so the entrypoint finds it (F15) |
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:318:Resolved secret values must never be persisted. jackin❯ already resolves 1Password references, operator env, and tokens fresh on every launch and passes them straight to `docker run` as env — they are never written to the manifest or data dir, and this design must not regress that. The recipe stores only the **reference** (`op://vault/item/field`, the env-var name, the GitHub auth mode); restore re-runs `op read` and re-resolves tokens with the operator's current access. Caching the resolved value would turn the per-instance data dir into a plaintext secret store — a security regression the design forbids.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/agents/orchestration/recovery/session-keep-and-resume-design.mdx:323:Why the ladder prefers reuse: some agent state is bind-mounted to the host and survives `docker rm`, and some lives only in the container's writable layer and does not.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:2:title: "Apple Container Backend — Architecture and Design"
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:3:description: Defines the Apple Container backend architecture, CLI integration, manifest model, telemetry, and security boundaries.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:10:Apple Container fits the backend abstraction through its CLI, but rootless DinD and mount behavior remain empirical gates.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:12:This design record covers the runtime stack, CLI boundary, manifest schema, and telemetry implemented by <RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/apple_container.rs" /> and <RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime-apple-container-client/src/apple_container_client.rs" />. experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:16:Apple Container (`apple/container`, macOS 26 Tahoe) gives each role container its own dedicated Linux VM via Virtualization.framework, natively on Apple Silicon, installed separately through Apple.s signed package or Homebrew. It is the primary path to close the **kernel boundary gap**: today all jackin❯ sessions share a single OrbStack Linux VM kernel, so a kernel exploit in one session can in principle reach other sessions. Apple Container gives each session its own kernel.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:22:  → apple/container VM (own kernel, Virtualization.framework)

Unknown unrelated metadata remains additive. `[]` is the only valid empty inventory; malformed output and unsuccessful list commands remain errors.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:97:Each apple/container VM gets its own dedicated IP address via vmnet — no port mapping needed. The optional field means old manifests deserialize unchanged. `INSTANCE_MANIFEST_VERSION` bumped from `1` to `2` to carry this.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:167:- **bollard-style typed Rust client**: rejected — Apple Container's programmatic surface is Swift XPC-native, not a Rust or REST API, so a bollard-equivalent typed client isn't available; shelling out to the `container` CLI is the only integration path for V1.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:168:- **Static `JACKIN_CAPSULE_FORCE_DAEMON` Dockerfile `ENV`**: rejected — would break the Docker backend, where capsule IS PID 1 and client-mode invocations (e.g. `jackin-capsule status`) must not enter daemon mode. The env var is injected only as a `-e` flag on the apple-container launch path, and <RepoFile path="crates/apps/jackin-capsule/src/main.rs" /> additionally gates on `forced_daemon_mode`/`is_daemon_entrypoint_args` so an inherited env var in `container exec` children (which also see the VM's env) cannot capture a client-mode invocation into daemon mode.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:173:The [roadmap item](/roadmap/apple-container-backend/) owns delivery. This page preserves the independent evidence and contract gates:
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:175:- **Empirical compatibility.** Phase 0 validation covers real macOS 26 ARM hardware with apple/container 1.0+: CLI/hardware/OS prerequisites, `JACKIN_CAPSULE_FORCE_DAEMON=1` non-PID-1 daemon activation, role image boot, `container exec -it` PTY, workspace bind mounts, `--cap-add CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, rootless DinD (`docker build`, Compose, Testcontainers), cold-start latency, and DNS stability after sleep/wake. Failure of rootless DinD preserves [smolvm](/research/platform/isolation/smolvm-backend/) as the fallback candidate.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-backend.mdx:183:Apple Container does not support `--privileged`. The current jackin❯ DinD sidecar requires it and cannot run inside an apple/container VM unchanged. Rootless DinD with specific `--cap-add` grants is the compatibility path. The [Docker runtime hardening contract](/roadmap/docker-runtime-hardening-contract/) must establish whether apple/container allows `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` for mount namespaces and whether `docker build`, Compose, and Testcontainers work inside the VM. Failure means Apple Container cannot support full role workflows; [smolvm](/research/platform/isolation/smolvm-backend/) remains the documented fallback with a constrained Docker-in-VM path.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:11:**Verification cutoff:** 2026-06-10. experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:17:1. **Apple Container 1.0 and Sandboxy support the four-layer architecture.** Sandboxy runs Claude Code inside a per-session lightweight VM behind a host-side network allowlist proxy with CPU and memory limits plus explicit mounts. It is an experimental example, but it independently demonstrates the VM-boundary, egress, resource, and mount controls required by jackin❯.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:18:2. **Apple Container 1.0 exposes the policy surface jackin❯ needs to evaluate:** resource limits, reduced and explicit capabilities, read-only mounts/rootfs, tmpfs, SSH forwarding, port and socket publication, internal networks, DNS controls, Rosetta, stop signals, structured inspection, and versioned system configuration.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:21:5. **smolvm 1.0.1 is a credible fallback.** The Rust, Apache-2.0 project provides VM fork, elastic memory through virtio-balloon, deny-by-default networking with `--allow-host`, and host-custodied SSH-agent forwarding. Apple Container remains the primary because of platform ownership and its vminitd attach contract; smolvm supplies useful comparison evidence for egress and memory reclaim.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:22:6. **Installation and DNS behavior are durable constraints**: apple/container is a separate open-source tool installed through a signed `.pkg` or Homebrew, and the sleep/wake DNS hiccup has no upstream fix through 1.0.0. The DNS health check therefore remains load-bearing.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:182:### 6.5 Verdict: Apple Container vs OrbStack (and the rest) — explicit answer
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/isolation/apple-container-1-0-landscape.mdx:184:**Apple Container is the strongest jackin❯ Layer 2 backend.** OrbStack 2.2 improves productivity isolation but retains a shared kernel. apple/container 1.0.0 provides a per-container VM, sub-second start, structured CLI output, capability controls, host-enforced read-only mounts, internal networks, and vminitd gRPC/vsock attach. Docker Sandboxes remains a useful benchmark rather than an integrable backend because its runner is closed, paid, and lacks an API. smolvm remains a credible fallback with elastic memory, CLI-native egress allowlists, and VM fork support; those capabilities inform the jackin❯ design without displacing Apple Container as the primary backend.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/isolation-architecture/agent-isolation-architecture/01-threat-and-platform-evidence.mdx:81:**Apple Container framework** (macOS 26 Tahoe, WWDC 2025, `apple/container` 1.0): each OCI container runs in its own lightweight VM via Virtualization.framework on Apple Silicon. It provides a per-container kernel, vmnet IP, OCI-compatible lifecycle, and `vminitd` gRPC/vsock process control. The signed package or Homebrew install is separate from macOS.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/isolation-architecture/agent-isolation-architecture/01-threat-and-platform-evidence.mdx:83:**Key finding for Apple Container**: `--privileged` is not supported. The documented alternative is `--cap-add <CAP>` for specific capabilities. This means the current jackin❯ DinD sidecar (which requires `--privileged`) cannot run inside an apple/container VM without changes. Rootless DinD — which replaces `--privileged` with specific capability grants — is the compatibility path, but requires the [Docker runtime hardening contract](/roadmap/docker-runtime-hardening-contract/) work to complete first.

experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:22:`docker pull` of the same reference confirms the cause is authentication, not staleness:
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:28:jackin❯ never sends registry credentials when pulling. The pull path in <RepoFile path="crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs">crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs</RepoFile> calls bollard `create_image` with the `RegistryAuth` argument hard-coded to `None`. Because the freshness check in <RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/image.rs">crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/image.rs</RepoFile> treats *any* pull failure as "image is stale", an auth failure is indistinguishable from a genuinely outdated image — so jackin❯ falls through to a full workspace rebuild from the Dockerfile (13+ minutes in the report above) and never tells the operator the real problem was a missing login.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:52:- **Consolidates a scattered surface.** Registry auth, agent tokens, the GitHub token, and 1Password references are today separate concepts in separate places. A configurations layer is where they converge, and it interlocks with [container credential exposure](/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/container-credential-exposure/) (resolved secrets must not leak into image layers, `docker inspect`, or logs) and the [workspace registry cache](/roadmap/workspace-registry-cache/) (a pull-through cache still needs upstream credentials).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:56:The structural move is a configurations layer: a list of named, scoped endpoint configurations, each carrying a credential sourced through the [`CredentialSource`](/roadmap/credential-source-pattern/) enum (literal, env, command, `op://`, OS keychain, and — new — Docker credential store/helper and an ECR provider). Registry pull-auth is the first consumer: resolve the matching configuration for a `published_image` reference, hand bollard real credentials, and distinguish auth failure from staleness so the operator gets an actionable message instead of a silent rebuild.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:58:### Pull through bollard, not the docker CLI
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:60:The pull path must stay in-process via the bollard API, not shell out to the `docker` CLI. The CLI offers no way to hold a session or inject ad-hoc credentials per pull; bollard does, and jackin❯ already calls it. This was verified against the pinned `bollard 0.21.0`:
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:62:- `create_image(options, body, credentials: Option<DockerCredentials>)` — the third argument is the injection point. Today it is `None` in <RepoFile path="crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs">crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs</RepoFile>. bollard base64-encodes the credentials into the `X-Registry-Auth` header itself — the same mechanism the CLI uses internally.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:63:- `bollard::auth::DockerCredentials { username, password, auth, email, serveraddress, identitytoken, registrytoken }` — all `Option<String>`. (Note: the type is `DockerCredentials`, not `RegistryAuth`.)
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:80:`pull_image(image)` parses the host from the reference, asks the provider, and hands bollard fresh `DockerCredentials`. Because resolution happens at pull time, a refreshed token is simply "the provider returned a newer value this call." Resolving at startup instead would capture a token that expires mid-session — the failure mode to avoid.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:113:Before committing to the design, a ~30-line throwaway (no jackin❯ integration) proves the whole approach: obtain an ECR token (`aws ecr get-login-password` or `aws-sdk-ecr`), build a `DockerCredentials { username: "AWS", password: token, serveraddress: <registry>, .. }`, and call `create_image(.., Some(creds))` to pull a private ECR image. If that pulls clean, bollard + in-process credentials + per-pull resolution is validated end to end.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:143:When an image reference matches no `[[registries]]` entry, jackin❯ falls back to the host's `~/.docker/config.json`, honoring `credsStore` and `credHelpers` (via the [`docker_credential`](https://github.com/keirlawson/docker_credential) crate). Why: this is exactly how docker, buildkit, and skopeo already behave, so it is least-surprise; an operator who has run `docker login` or installed `docker-credential-ecr-login` gets working private pulls with zero jackin❯ configuration — the "provide once" promise. Reading `~/.docker/config.json` is a host read, allowed under the [no-silent-host-writes rule](/roadmap/sensitive-boundary-code-health/) (no opt-in needed; no host writes). Resolution stays host-side and credentials are handed only to the docker daemon via bollard's auth header — they never enter the container env or image layers (container credential exposure).
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:177:One `BollardDockerClient` (host daemon via `DOCKER_HOST`/defaults, <RepoFile path="crates/adapters/jackin-docker/src/docker_client.rs">docker_client.rs</RepoFile>) drives every pull — the published-image freshness check (<RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/image.rs">image.rs</RepoFile>) and the DinD sidecar image pull (<RepoFile path="crates/services/jackin-runtime/src/runtime/launch/launch_dind.rs">launch_dind.rs</RepoFile>). So credential resolution threads into `pull_image` itself — a single injection point that covers role images and the DinD base image (which may also be private). Why this is clean: creds are produced host-side and handed only to the docker daemon via bollard's `X-Registry-Auth` header; they never cross into a container's env or image layers, satisfying container-credential-exposure goals without extra plumbing. The provider is constructed once and passed down to the docker client.
experiments/full-refactor-20261024/source/jackin/docs/content/research/platform/security/credential-exposure/registry-configurations-design.mdx:206:- Reuse-vs-build for the mechanics: bollard for the pull is settled; for sourcing, choose between the [`docker_credential`](https://github.com/keirlawson/docker_credential) crate, native [`aws-sdk-ecr`](https://crates.io/crates/aws-sdk-ecr), shelling out to the credential helper, and (if pulls ever move off bollard) [`oci-client`/`rust-oci-client`](https://github.com/oras-project/rust-oci-client) — per the "prefer maintained crates" rule.
